## Civil rights and constitutional remedies

### Individual-capacity liability for official conduct under §1983

**Governing question:** Whether use of official authority prevents a state officer from being a §1983 person when sued individually.

**Current rule:** A state officer sued in an individual capacity is a person under §1983 even when the challenged act was undertaken through official authority; sovereign immunity does not independently bar a judgment payable by the officer personally. The plaintiff must prove the officer's own federal violation under color of law, and applicable personal immunities remain available. The capacity in which the officer is sued, rather than whether the conduct was official, distinguishes this action from a claim against the State or an official-capacity damages claim.

**Authority by component:**

- Personhood, capacity distinction, personal-payment immunity limit, and retained defenses: [Hafer v. Melo — Individual-capacity §1983 coverage of official conduct](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Hafer_v_Melo_merits_1991-11-05.md), November 5, 1991; O'Connor's unanimous participating opinion.

**Present operation effective:** *Hafer*, November 5, 1991.

**Holdings navigation:** [Hafer v. Melo, 502 U.S. 21 (1991) — Individual-capacity liability](C:/Users/alexl/Projects/scotus-career/terms/OT1992/close/HOLDINGS.candidate.md#hafer-v-melo-502-us-21-1991).

### Municipal responsibility and the underlying federal deprivation

**Governing question:** Whether a municipal employee must prove an additional freestanding abuse of governmental power to recover under §1983.

**Current rule:** A municipal employee who proves deprivation of a federal right under color of state law and the required municipal responsibility need not prove an additional, independent abuse-of-governmental-power element. The federal deprivation and its attribution to municipal policy or custom remain separate required inquiries. Employment alone establishes neither municipal liability nor a substantive right; *Canton*'s training analysis supplies attribution and causation, not the underlying constitutional guarantee.

**Authority by component:**

- Rejection of the additional element and separation of deprivation from attribution: [Collins v. City of Harker Heights — No extra governmental-abuse element under §1983](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Collins_v_City_of_Harker_Heights_merits_1992-02-26.md), February 26, 1992; Part A of Stevens's opinion, joined by all nine Justices.

**Present operation effective:** *Collins*, February 26, 1992.

**Holdings navigation:** [Collins v. City of Harker Heights, 503 U.S. 115 (1992) — Municipal liability and public employment](C:/Users/alexl/Projects/scotus-career/terms/OT1992/close/HOLDINGS.candidate.md#collins-v-city-of-harker-heights-503-us-115-1992).

### Voluntary public employment and substantive workplace safety duties

**Governing question:** Whether a voluntary public-employment relationship creates a general substantive-due-process duty to provide workplace safety.

**Current rule:** Voluntary municipal employment does not itself create the custody or deprivation of liberty that supports affirmative constitutional duties of care, and substantive due process imposes no independent general obligation to provide such employees a workplace free from unreasonable risks. A state-law safety duty, even assuming it creates a protected liberty interest, does not convert its breach alone into a substantive constitutional wrong. The underlying federal right and constitutional arbitrariness remain distinct from state-law entitlement and municipal attribution.

**Authority by component:**

- No employment-based general safety duty or custody: [Collins v. City of Harker Heights — Voluntary public employment creates no general constitutional safety duty](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Collins_v_City_of_Harker_Heights_merits_1992-02-26.md), February 26, 1992; Part B of Stevens's unanimous opinion.
- State entitlement does not alone establish arbitrary deprivation: [Collins — These pleaded omissions do not establish constitutional arbitrariness](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Collins_v_City_of_Harker_Heights_merits_1992-02-26.md), February 26, 1992; Part C joined by White, Blackmun, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, and Thomas with Stevens. Stone's distinct concurrence is not part of this component's authority.

**Present operation effective:** *Collins*, February 26, 1992.

**Open question:** Actual compulsion, deliberately inflicted bodily injury, procedural due process, and a properly alleged affirmative-danger claim are not resolved. *Collins* creates neither a general affirmative-danger test nor a categorical rejection of one.

**Holdings navigation:** [Collins v. City of Harker Heights, 503 U.S. 115 (1992) — Municipal liability and public employment](C:/Users/alexl/Projects/scotus-career/terms/OT1992/close/HOLDINGS.candidate.md#collins-v-city-of-harker-heights-503-us-115-1992).

### Compensatory remedies for intentional Title IX discrimination

**Governing question:** Whether remedial silence or Spending Clause notice bars compensatory damages in the established Title IX private action.

**Current rule:** When a cognizable federal statutory cause of action exists, courts may award appropriate relief absent clear congressional direction limiting it; recognizing relief does not itself create a new cause of action. Title IX's established private action permits compensatory damages for proved intentional discrimination, subject to proof of recipient responsibility, causation, and compensable loss. The Spending Clause notice principle does not categorically bar that compensation for intentional breach of Title IX's express nondiscrimination condition.

**Authority by component:**

- Appropriate relief in an existing action and availability of Title IX compensation: [Franklin v. Gwinnett County Public Schools — Compensatory relief in the established Title IX action](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Franklin_v_Gwinnett_County_Public_Schools_merits_1992-02-26.md), February 26, 1992.
- Rejection of the categorical funding-notice bar: [Franklin — Notice does not bar damages for the pleaded intentional violation](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Franklin_v_Gwinnett_County_Public_Schools_merits_1992-02-26.md), February 26, 1992. Both holdings rest on White's opinion joined by Stone, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, and Souter; Scalia and Thomas supply judgment votes only on a narrower statutory ground.

**Present operation effective:** *Franklin*, February 26, 1992.

**Open question:** The decision supplies no final recipient-attribution rule, punitive remedy, liability for unintended discriminatory effects, or resolution of genuinely ambiguous funding conditions.

**Holdings navigation:** [Franklin v. Gwinnett County Public Schools, 503 U.S. 60 (1992) — Statutory discrimination remedies](C:/Users/alexl/Projects/scotus-career/terms/OT1992/close/HOLDINGS.candidate.md#franklin-v-gwinnett-county-public-schools-503-us-60-1992).

### Private enforcement of Adoption Assistance Act reasonable-efforts and reporting clauses

**Governing question:** Private enforcement of 42 U.S.C. §671(a)(15)'s reasonable-efforts clause and §671(a)(9)'s reporting clause through §1983 or an action implied directly under the Act.

**Current rule:** Read within the Act's plan and administrative-enforcement structure, §671(a)(15) creates no individual right enforceable under §1983 to compel the asserted reasonable efforts to prevent removal or reunify families. An approved plan's commitment, §671(a)(3)'s statewide-operation requirement and caseworker deficiencies do not supply that missing right. Nor does §671(a)(15) imply a separate private action: beneficial purpose does not establish congressional intent to create the requested remedy. Section 671(a)(9) requires a plan to provide reporting to the appropriate court or law-enforcement agency when a state agency has reason to believe a child's home or institution is unsuitable because of neglect, abuse or exploitation and the child's care is paid for in whole or in part under Title IV-E or IV-B. That administrative direction does not create the asserted private §1983 right or an implied action to obtain caseworker relief. The §1983-right and implied-action inquiries remain distinct; failure to imply an action does not bar an otherwise available enacted §1983 remedy.

**Authority by component:**
- No individual §671(a)(15) right: [Suter v. Artist M.](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Suter_v_Artist_M_merits_1992-03-25.md), March 25, 1992, “Section 671(a)(15) supplies no individual reasonable-efforts right enforceable under §1983,” Part II.
- No separate implied (15) action: the same record, “Section 671(a)(15) supplies no separate implied action,” Part III.
- Reporting trigger and absence of asserted §1983 right: the same record, “The asserted §671(a)(9) reporting claim is not enforceable through §1983,” Part IV-A.
- No implied reporting action: the same record, “The asserted §671(a)(9) claim supplies no separate implied action,” Part IV-B. White for O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter and Thomas supplies six controlling joins for each component.

**Present operation effective:** Suter, March 25, 1992.

**Exceptions and limits:** No general rule makes state-plan provisions unenforceable. Administrative funding review does not necessarily foreclose an otherwise existing §1983 right, and the State's administrative obligations remain. Appropriate relief presupposes a cognizable action and cannot create the missing right or action.

**Open question:** Private enforceability of §671(a)(16), read with §675(1) and §675(5)(B), remains undecided, as do the preserved claim's merits and proper relief.

**Holdings navigation:** [Suter v. Artist M., 503 U.S. 347 (1992) — Child-welfare statutory enforcement](C:/Users/alexl/Projects/scotus-career/terms/OT1992/close/HOLDINGS.candidate.md#suter-v-artist-m-503-us-347-1992-1).

### Private use of state seizure procedures and qualified immunity

**Governing question:** Whether private litigants using state replevin, garnishment or attachment procedures receive public-official immunity under Harlow.

**Current rule:** Private litigants invoking those procedures do not receive Harlow's objective immunity from suit merely because qualifying joint action is attributable to the State under §1983. State attribution and immunity eligibility remain separate; reasonable reliance on legislation does not itself establish this immunity. Each defendant's attribution and other liability predicates still require independent determination.

**Authority by component:**
- Private-immunity exclusion and separate attribution: [Wyatt v. Cole, “Private litigants and immunity from suit”](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Wyatt_v_Cole_merits_1992-05-18.md), May 18, 1992; O'Connor, joined in full by White, Blackmun, Stevens, Scalia and Kennedy (six); Stone adds a join only for replevin and the reservations (seven at that scope).

**Present operation effective:** Wyatt v. Cole, May 18, 1992.

**Open question:** The existence and scope of a distinct good-faith or probable-cause defense, any additional plaintiff's elements and allocation of any such burden remain undecided. Eligible public officials' immunity is unchanged.

**Holdings navigation:** [Wyatt v. Cole, 504 U.S. 158 (1992) — Section 1983 immunity](C:/Users/alexl/Projects/scotus-career/terms/OT1992/close/HOLDINGS.candidate.md#wyatt-v-cole-504-us-158-1992).

### Municipal civil-rights pleading under the Federal Rules

**Governing question:** What instructions govern municipal civil-rights pleading under the Federal Rules?

**Current rule:** Courts may not impose a judge-created heightened factual-particularity requirement on municipal §1983 complaints beyond the then-operative Federal Rules. Rule 8 governs; Rule 9(b)'s fraud-or-mistake categories do not add municipal liability. Monell and Canton retain their substantive attribution and causation requirements. Rule 11, discovery control and Rule 56 remain distinct. No individual-officer pleading standard is decided.

**Authority by component:**

- The Federal Rules supply the pleading standard for municipal § 1983 claims: [Leatherman v. Tarrant County Narcotics Intelligence and Coordination Unit — The Federal Rules supply the pleading standard for municipal § 1983 claims](C:/Users/alexl/Projects/scotus-career/terms/OT1992/records/leatherman-v-tarrant-county-merits-1993-03-03.md), 1993-03-03. Stone's Opinion of the Court, joined in full by White, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter and Thomas; unanimous controlling authority.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-03-03; Leatherman v. Tarrant County Narcotics Intelligence and Coordination Unit, for the components identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** [Leatherman v. Tarrant County Narcotics Intelligence and Coordination Unit, 507 U.S. 163 (1993)](C:/Users/alexl/Projects/scotus-career/terms/OT1992/close/HOLDINGS.candidate.md#leatherman-v-tarrant-county-narcotics-intelligence-and-coordination-unit-507-us-163-1993).

### Private-conspiracy deprivation claims under §1985(3)

**Governing question:** What instructions govern private-conspiracy deprivation claims under §1985(3)?

**Current rule:** A purely private conspiracy under §1985(3)'s deprivation clause must aim to interfere with a right protected against private as well as official encroachment; incidental effects do not suffice. Interstate-travel interference must be a conscious objective, not simply an effect on persons who traveled. Constitutional abortion liberty does not itself protect against purely private obstruction without necessary state involvement or another privately protected right. The full nonracial-class boundary, whether women qualify and whether abortion opposition proves or excludes sex-based animus remain unresolved. The separate hindrance clause is not construed, and no categorical intrastate-obstruction rule is adopted.

**Authority by component:**

- Private conspiracy requires purposeful interference with a privately protected right: [Bray v. Alexandria Women’s Health Clinic — Private conspiracy requires purposeful interference with a privately protected right](C:/Users/alexl/Projects/scotus-career/terms/OT1992/records/Bray_v_Alexandria_Womens_Health_Clinic_merits_1993-01-13.md), 1993-01-13. Scalia Part II, joined by Stone-Zsela, White, Kennedy and Thomas: five direct supporters. Souter separately agrees with rejection of this theory without a formal join.
- Constitutional abortion liberty does not itself supply the private-right element: [Bray v. Alexandria Women’s Health Clinic — Constitutional abortion liberty does not itself supply the private-right element](C:/Users/alexl/Projects/scotus-career/terms/OT1992/records/Bray_v_Alexandria_Womens_Health_Clinic_merits_1993-01-13.md), 1993-01-13. Scalia Part II, joined by Stone-Zsela, White, Kennedy and Thomas: five direct supporters of this distinct sufficient ground addressing the alternative privacy theory. Souter separately accepts Scott’s private-right limitation.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-01-13; Bray v. Alexandria Women’s Health Clinic, for the components identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** [Bray v. Alexandria Women’s Health Clinic, 506 U.S. 263 (1993)](C:/Users/alexl/Projects/scotus-career/terms/OT1992/close/HOLDINGS.candidate.md#bray-v-alexandria-womens-health-clinic-506-us-263-1993); [Bray v. Alexandria Women’s Health Clinic, 506 U.S. 263 (1993)](C:/Users/alexl/Projects/scotus-career/terms/OT1992/close/HOLDINGS.candidate.md#bray-v-alexandria-womens-health-clinic-506-us-263-1993-1).

### Ministerial court reporting and absolute judicial immunity

**Governing question:** What instructions govern ministerial court reporting and absolute judicial immunity?

**Current rule:** Accurate recording and production of a verbatim transcript are ministerial functions and do not carry absolute judicial immunity merely because they are important or integral to adjudication. The claimant of immunity must establish its historical and functional justification; discretionary adjudication supplies a different function. Denial of absolute immunity establishes neither a cause of action nor liability, and other defenses remain open.

**Authority by component:**

- The ministerial transcript function does not carry absolute judicial immunity: [Antoine v. Byers & Anderson, Inc. — The ministerial transcript function does not carry absolute judicial immunity](C:/Users/alexl/Projects/scotus-career/terms/OT1992/records/antoine_merits_1993-06-07.md), 1993-06-07. Stevens’s Opinion of the Court; Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (9 Justices). Direct majority support at this level of generality; no Marks synthesis.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-06-07; Antoine v. Byers & Anderson, Inc., for the components identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** [Antoine v. Byers & Anderson, Inc., 508 U.S. 429 (1993)](C:/Users/alexl/Projects/scotus-career/terms/OT1992/close/HOLDINGS.candidate.md#antoine-v-byers--anderson-inc-508-us-429-1993).

### Prosecutorial investigation, advocacy and press statements

**Governing question:** What instructions govern prosecutorial investigation, advocacy and press statements?

**Current rule:** Ordinary investigative work to identify a perpetrator before probable cause has no absolute prosecutorial immunity for alleged fabrication; later courtroom use does not retrospectively transform it into advocacy. Genuine evaluation and preparation in an advocate's role remain protected; neither all pre-indictment nor all post-indictment acts share one classification. Statements to the press announcing an indictment also lack absolute prosecutorial immunity merely because they concern prosecution or cause injury through subsequent proceedings. Qualified immunity, an actionable constitutional deprivation and other liability predicates remain separate.

**Authority by component:**

- Investigative fabrication is not protected by absolute prosecutorial immunity: [Buckley v. Fitzsimmons — Investigative fabrication is not protected by absolute prosecutorial immunity](C:/Users/alexl/Projects/scotus-career/terms/OT1992/records/Buckley_v_Fitzsimmons_merits_1993-06-24.md), 1993-06-24. Stevens’s Opinion of the Court, this proposition; Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, O’Connor, Scalia, Thomas (6 Justices). Direct majority support at the stated level of generality; no Marks aggregation.
- A press conference does not carry an advocate’s absolute immunity: [Buckley v. Fitzsimmons — A press conference does not carry an advocate’s absolute immunity](C:/Users/alexl/Projects/scotus-career/terms/OT1992/records/Buckley_v_Fitzsimmons_merits_1993-06-24.md), 1993-06-24. Stevens’s Opinion of the Court, this proposition; Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, Stevens, O’Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (9 Justices). Direct majority support at the stated level of generality; no Marks aggregation.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-06-24; Buckley v. Fitzsimmons, for the components identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** [Buckley v. Fitzsimmons, 509 U.S. 259 (1993)](C:/Users/alexl/Projects/scotus-career/terms/OT1992/close/HOLDINGS.candidate.md#buckley-v-fitzsimmons-509-us-259-1993).

## Criminal procedure, evidence, and punishment

### Confrontation and firmly rooted spontaneous-declaration and medical-treatment exceptions

**Governing question:** Whether production of the declarant or proof of unavailability is a prerequisite to admission under these accepted hearsay exceptions.

**Current rule:** When statements satisfy the firmly rooted spontaneous-declaration or medical-treatment exception, the Confrontation Clause does not require the prosecution first to produce the declarant or establish unavailability. The exception's actual foundations remain necessary; naming an exception cannot establish a disputed foundation or validate every police interview. The Clause is not categorically confined to formal affidavits or equivalent trial substitutes, so that proposed limitation cannot supply an alternative ground for admission. The received exception-sensitive confrontation framework remains in force.

**Authority by component:**

- Exception-specific absence of a production or unavailability gate: [White v. Illinois — No universal production or unavailability prerequisite](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/White_v_Illinois_merits_1992-01-15.md), January 15, 1992; Part I of Stone's opinion joined by all nine Justices.
- Rejection of a formal-accusation-only boundary: [White — The government's categorical definition of witness is rejected](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/White_v_Illinois_merits_1992-01-15.md), January 15, 1992; Part II joined by White, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, and Souter with Stone. Scalia and Thomas do not join that component.

**Present operation effective:** *White v. Illinois*, January 15, 1992.

**Exceptions and limits:** Former-testimony unavailability analysis, constitutional reliability outside firmly rooted exceptions, and safeguards for altered presentation of trial witnesses remain distinct. No later testimonial or primary-purpose test is adopted.

**Holdings navigation:** [White v. Illinois, 502 U.S. 346 (1992) — Confrontation and hearsay](C:/Users/alexl/Projects/scotus-career/terms/OT1992/close/HOLDINGS.candidate.md#white-v-illinois-502-us-346-1992).

### Physical injury in a nontrivial malicious custodial beating

**Governing question:** Whether significant physical injury is an additional necessary element where a restrained convicted prisoner suffers a nontrivial malicious beating satisfying *Whitley*.

**Current rule:** A nontrivial beating inflicted maliciously and without disciplinary justification on a restrained convicted prisoner violates the Eighth Amendment despite limited resulting physical injury when the established conduct satisfies *Whitley*'s demanding malicious-and-sadistic inquiry. Injury remains relevant evidence and bears on compensation; accidental harm or merely technical battery is insufficient. This rule identifies a sufficient constitutional violation and does not impose a universal mental-state standard for all prison-force claims.

**Authority by component:**

- Rejection of a significant-injury floor at the adopted narrow scope: [Hudson v. McMillian — Limited injury does not defeat this established nontrivial malicious beating](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Hudson_v_McMillian_merits_1992-02-25.md), February 25, 1992; Part A of O'Connor's opinion joined by Stone, White, Stevens, Kennedy, and Souter. The proposed universal *Whitley* extension has only four votes and is noncontrolling.

**Present operation effective:** *Hudson*, February 25, 1992.

**Open question:** A comprehensive mental-state rule for other force settings, psychological injury alone, and a general definition of punishment covering isolated unauthorized assaults remain unresolved. Medical-care, confinement-condition, personal-defense, and compensation rules remain distinct.

**Holdings navigation:** [Hudson v. McMillian, 503 U.S. 1 (1992) — Prisoners’ rights](C:/Users/alexl/Projects/scotus-career/terms/OT1992/close/HOLDINGS.candidate.md#hudson-v-mcmillian-503-us-1-1992).

### Review of Guidelines departures resting on invalid grounds

**Governing question:** When a reviewing court may affirm a Guidelines departure supported in part by an impermissible factor under 18 U.S.C. §3742(f).

**Current rule:** A departure based on a factor forbidden by the Commission, or already fully considered in the applicable range, is an incorrect Guidelines application under §3742(f)(1). An express prohibitory policy statement is authoritative guidance in applying the relevant Guideline; the arrest record itself cannot supply a departure ground. Once the challenger establishes reliance on an invalid factor, remand is required unless the party defending the sentence persuades the reviewing court, from the whole record, that the district court would have imposed the same sentence without it. The challenger need not prove that the error determined the sentence. Even if that error was harmless, §3742(f)(2) independently requires a reasonable departure extent justified by valid grounds the sentencing court actually identified. That the court could lawfully have imposed the sentence does not establish either actual causal harmlessness or permission for an appellate court to invent departure grounds.

**Authority by component:**
- Invalid-factor error and express policy prohibition: [Williams v. United States](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Williams_v_United_States_merits_1992-03-09.md), March 9, 1992, “An impermissible departure ground is a Guidelines application error.”
- Same-sentence inquiry and burden sequence: the same record, “Harmlessness requires the same sentence without the invalid factor.”
- Independent reasonableness inquiry and actual-ground limit: the same record, “Departure reasonableness remains a separate condition of affirmance.” O'Connor's opinion has seven joins at each level: Stone, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Souter and Thomas.

**Present operation effective:** Williams, March 9, 1992.

**Exceptions and limits:** This is nonconstitutional error governed by Rule 52(a); no Chapman beyond-reasonable-doubt burden is adopted. The decision does not make every policy statement binding for every purpose or decide whether old nonsimilar convictions support a departure.

**Holdings navigation:** [Williams v. United States, 503 U.S. 193 (1992) — Guidelines departure review](C:/Users/alexl/Projects/scotus-career/terms/OT1992/close/HOLDINGS.candidate.md#williams-v-united-states-503-us-193-1992).

### Adult Guidelines ceiling for juvenile detention

**Governing question:** The maximum detention authorized by 18 U.S.C. §5037(c)(1)(B) for a juvenile compared with the analogous adult offender and offense.

**Current rule:** The maximum authorized adult sentence used in §5037(c)(1)(B) includes the applicable adult Guidelines ceiling, subject to a properly supported lawful departure. It supplies an upper limit only: the Guidelines minimum does not become a juvenile floor, juvenile discretion below the cap remains, and the other statutory limits continue to apply. The construction does not require plenary adult Guidelines sentencing of the juvenile.

**Authority by component:**
- Upper-limit construction, departure qualification and preserved juvenile discretion: [United States v. R. L. C.](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/United_States_v_RLC_merits_1992-03-24.md), March 24, 1992, “The juvenile ceiling incorporates the lawful adult Guidelines maximum”; Souter's Parts I, II-A and III for Stone, White, Stevens, Scalia, Kennedy and Thomas.

**Present operation effective:** R. L. C., March 24, 1992.

**Related current rules:** “Review of Guidelines departures resting on invalid grounds” supplies the lawful-departure limitation; R. L. C. approves no new departure ground.

**Holdings navigation:** [United States v. R. L. C., 503 U.S. 291 (1992) — Juvenile sentencing](C:/Users/alexl/Projects/scotus-career/terms/OT1992/close/HOLDINGS.candidate.md#united-states-v-r-l-c-503-us-291-1992-1).

### Initial calculation of federal detention credit

**Governing question:** Who initially computes credit under 18 U.S.C. §3585(b), when the computation occurs, and the treatment of time credited to another sentence.

**Current rule:** The Attorney General through the Bureau of Prisons must initially calculate §3585(b) credit after the federal sentence commences; a district court may not calculate that credit at sentencing. Time already credited against another sentence when the federal term begins cannot also receive federal credit merely because the federal sentencing hearing occurred first. Administrative review and proper subsequent judicial review remain available.

**Authority by component:**
- Actor, timing and review: [United States v. Wilson](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/United_States_v_Wilson_merits_1992-03-24.md), March 24, 1992, “BOP makes the initial credit computation after commencement.”
- Commencement-based no-double-credit construction: the same record, “Credit already applied to another sentence cannot be counted again.” Thomas's opinion for Stone, Blackmun, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy and Souter controls both components.

**Present operation effective:** Wilson, March 24, 1992.

**Open question:** The decision does not define every form of “official detention.”

**Holdings navigation:** [United States v. Wilson, 503 U.S. 329 (1992) — Detention credit](C:/Users/alexl/Projects/scotus-career/terms/OT1992/close/HOLDINGS.candidate.md#united-states-v-wilson-503-us-329-1992).

### Successive prosecutions, evidentiary overlap and criminal contempt

**Governing question:** When evidentiary overlap, conspiracy or completed criminal contempt bars a successive prosecution.

**Current rule:** Evidentiary use of an uncharged crime does not itself place the defendant in jeopardy for that crime. A conspiracy and its substantive objects remain distinct offenses; prosecuting the substantive crime does not bar prosecution of the agreement merely because acts help prove both. Grady's essential-conduct rule remains operative and does not displace the established agreement/substantive-offense distinction or become a same-evidence or same-transaction rule. Ordinary evidentiary restrictions retain their separate force. Beyond Blockburger, Grady bars a later prosecution when establishing an essential element requires proof of conduct constituting an offense already prosecuted; evidence reuse or a common episode alone does not suffice. Completed nonsummary criminal contempt places the accused in criminal jeopardy despite private initiation or a protective purpose. When contempt necessarily prosecutes a substantive offense forbidden by the order, the Government cannot prosecute that same offense again. Coercive civil sanctions, summary courtroom contempt and separate sovereigns remain outside Dixon. Foster II-V may proceed under its case-specific judgment, but no controlling rationale governs those counts: the Blockburger-only/overruling and special judicial-order rationales are incompatible, not logical subsets. Grady survives without an invented general contempt exception.

**Authority by component:**
- Evidentiary use is not prior prosecution: [United States v. Felix](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/United_States_v_Felix_merits_1992-03-25.md), March 25, 1992, holding 1, White's unanimous Part I.
- Grady's relation to the distinct-agreement rule: the same record, holding 2, “Agreement remains a distinct offense,” White's Part II for Stone, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter and Thomas. The two narrower judgment votes do not limit the seven-Justice rationale.

- Nonsummary punitive contempt triggers double-jeopardy protection: [United States v. Dixon — Nonsummary punitive contempt triggers double-jeopardy protection](C:/Users/alexl/Projects/scotus-career/terms/OT1992/records/United_States_v_Dixon_merits_1993-06-28.md), June 28, 1993. White’s Opinion of the Court, this proposition; Stone-Zsela, White, Stevens, O’Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (8 Justices). Direct majority support at the stated level of generality; no Marks aggregation.
- Previously prosecuted necessary offenses cannot be tried again: [United States v. Dixon — Previously prosecuted necessary offenses cannot be tried again](C:/Users/alexl/Projects/scotus-career/terms/OT1992/records/United_States_v_Dixon_merits_1993-06-28.md), June 28, 1993. White’s Opinion of the Court, this proposition; Stone-Zsela, White, Stevens, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter (6 Justices). Direct majority support at the stated level of generality; no Marks aggregation.
- Grady remains governing successive-prosecution law: [United States v. Dixon — Grady remains governing successive-prosecution law](C:/Users/alexl/Projects/scotus-career/terms/OT1992/records/United_States_v_Dixon_merits_1993-06-28.md), June 28, 1993. Souter’s Opinion of the Court, this proposition; Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, Stevens, Souter (5 Justices). Direct majority support at the stated level of generality; no Marks aggregation.

**Present operation effective:** Dixon, June 28, 1993, for nonsummary criminal contempt and necessary-offense protection. Felix's evidence/conspiracy components remain effective March 25, 1992; Dixon retains Grady rather than replacing it.

**Holdings navigation:** [United States v. Felix, 503 U.S. 378 (1992) — Double jeopardy](C:/Users/alexl/Projects/scotus-career/terms/OT1992/close/HOLDINGS.candidate.md#united-states-v-felix-503-us-378-1992).

**Additional Holdings navigation:** [United States v. Dixon, 509 U.S. 688 (1993)](C:/Users/alexl/Projects/scotus-career/terms/OT1992/close/HOLDINGS.candidate.md#united-states-v-dixon-509-us-688-1993).

### Disclosure of known direct-negation evidence and supervisory dismissal

**Governing question:** A federal prosecutor's duty to disclose known evidence before seeking an indictment, and the distinct conditions permitting supervisory dismissal for nondisclosure.

**Current rule:** Before seeking an indictment, a federal prosecutor must present or otherwise disclose to the grand jury personally known, substantial evidence directly negating the charged offense when omission would materially distort the presentation. This requires neither a search for undiscovered favorable evidence nor presentation of every potential defense. Violation alone does not establish entitlement to dismissal. Supervisory authority permits dismissal without prejudice when the prosecutor withholds such personally known substantial direct-negation evidence, the omission materially distorts the presentation, the evidence would plainly preclude probable cause, and the violation substantially influenced the indictment or leaves grave doubt that it was free of that influence. Those additional conditions identify a sufficient, nonexclusive dismissal class: plain preclusion of probable cause is not part of the disclosure trigger, and the decision does not forbid relief in every other circumstance. The Government may seek a lawful new indictment.

**Authority by component:**
- Disclosure command, timing, recipient and material-distortion qualification: [United States v. Williams](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/United_States_v_Williams_merits_1992-05-04.md), May 4, 1992, “Disclosure of known evidence directly negating guilt.” The record's Marks determination identifies the shared rule of Stevens's Parts II–III, joined by Blackmun, O'Connor and Thomas, and Stone's separate merits opinion; Stone's material-distortion qualification remains operative.
- Sufficient dismissal class, independent prejudice and without-prejudice remedy: the same record, “Confined supervisory power over a materially misleading presentation.” The four-Justice direct-negation/plainly-no-probable-cause ground, with material distortion and charging prejudice, is the record's controlling logical subset of Stone's broader known-element-undermining/material-distortion rule with the same prejudice and remedy. Stone does not formally join the narrower qualification, and no single majority merits opinion exists.

**Present operation effective:** United States v. Williams, May 4, 1992.

**Exceptions and limits:** Costello's evidentiary-competence and sufficiency restrictions remain for otherwise lawful presentations. Calandra's grand-jury independence and freedom from trial evidence rules remain. Brady's trial obligation is not transplanted. The particular accounting evidence was outside the accepted authority-only question; affirmance creates no general factual conclusion that evidence bearing on intent necessarily satisfies the controlling sufficient dismissal class.

**Open question:** Applications outside the sufficient dismissal class, a constitutional disclosure duty, state grand juries, negligent discovery and unpresented immunity or fabrication claims remain unresolved.

**Holdings navigation:** [United States v. Williams, 504 U.S. 36 (1992) — Grand-jury presentation and supervisory dismissal](C:/Users/alexl/Projects/scotus-career/terms/OT1992/close/HOLDINGS.candidate.md#united-states-v-williams-504-us-36-1992-1).

### Equitable delay independently of a habeas procedural bar

**Governing question:** Whether an execution stay may lack equitable support even assuming the challenge is cognizable under §1983 and not barred as abuse of the writ.

**Current rule:** A court considering an execution stay may weigh the State's interest in enforcing its judgment and unjustified delay in presenting a long-available claim independently of a habeas procedural bar. A supported serious constitutional claim and the irreversible consequence of execution require consideration but do not alone establish entitlement to last-minute relief; later evidence strengthening the claim does not necessarily explain withholding the underlying challenge. Equitable vacatur on that ground may proceed while assuming an open §1983 channel and without adjudicating every McCleskey abuse element. The rule supplies a contextual equitable inquiry, not an automatic bar for late claims or an automatic capital stay.

**Authority by component:**
- Independent equitable ground, relevant considerations and procedural assumptions: [Gomez v. United States District Court](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Gomez_v_United_States_District_Court_application_to_vacate_stay_1992-04-21.md), April 21, 1992, “Equitable basis for vacating the execution stay,” per curiam for White, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter and Thomas, six votes. The unexplained postponement through four earlier habeas proceedings supplies the record application, not a minimum numerical prerequisite.

**Present operation effective:** Gomez, April 21, 1992.

**Exceptions and limits:** Delo's abuse-of-discretion stay review, Barefoot's substantial-ground requirements and McCleskey's rules within their proper habeas domain remain intact. Gomez does not prohibit later federal stays. The subsequent A-766 and A-768 unexplained orders create no reusable rationale; A-768's Harris-specific Court-authorization condition does not enlarge this general instruction.

**Open question:** The §1983/habeas boundary for execution-method relief, a full McCleskey application here, Younger abstention, final constitutionality of cyanide gas and a universal alternative-method requirement remain undecided.

**Holdings navigation:** [Gomez v. United States District Court for the Northern District of California, 503 U.S. 653 (1992) — Execution stays](C:/Users/alexl/Projects/scotus-career/terms/OT1992/close/HOLDINGS.candidate.md#gomez-v-united-states-district-court-for-the-northern-district-of-california-503-us-653-1992).

### Government motions and review of refused substantial-assistance relief

**Governing question:** Sentencing relief under 18 U.S.C. §3553(e) and USSG §5K1.1 when the Government has not moved on account of substantial assistance.

**Current rule:** A Government motion is the statutory trigger; useful assistance alone authorizes neither a departure nor a judicial order requiring a motion. The provisions give the Government power, rather than a duty, to move whenever cooperation proves useful. Courts may review a refusal and grant relief for unconstitutional purpose, including race or religion, and a defendant is entitled to relief when refusal is not rationally related to any legitimate governmental end. Useful assistance alone does not establish either defect because the Government may rationally weigh the costs and benefits of moving. Discovery or an evidentiary hearing requires a substantial threshold showing of improper motive; useful assistance, with or without generalized accusations, is insufficient.

**Authority by component:**
- Motion prerequisite and review for unconstitutional purpose: [Wade v. United States, “Government motion is the statutory trigger” and “Constitutional review of a refusal remains available”](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Wade_v_United_States_merits_1992-05-18.md), May 18, 1992; Souter Part A, joined by all other participating Justices (nine).
- Legitimate-end requirement and discovery threshold: Wade, “Refusals unrelated to a legitimate governmental end” and “Threshold for discovery or an evidentiary hearing,” May 18, 1992; Souter Part B, joined by White, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy and Thomas (eight).

**Present operation effective:** Wade v. United States, May 18, 1992.

**Open question:** Particular corrective remedies, enforcement of an established plea promise, independent operation of §3553(e) and §5K1.1, and departure amount remain unresolved by Wade. Its motion restriction is not classified as an Article III jurisdictional limit.

**Holdings navigation:** [Wade v. United States, 504 U.S. 181 (1992) — Substantial-assistance sentencing](C:/Users/alexl/Projects/scotus-career/terms/OT1992/close/HOLDINGS.candidate.md#wade-v-united-states-504-us-181-1992).

### Official-right extortion and payment for an official undertaking

**Governing question:** Required conduct and agreement for official-right extortion under 18 U.S.C. §1951.

**Current rule:** An official who obtains unauthorized money knowing that it is given in return for official acts need not independently initiate, demand or separately induce payment. Knowing acceptance in exchange for a specific requested exercise of official power is distinct from a lawful campaign contribution standing alone, even if its donor has pending business. McCormick's requirement that a campaign contribution be given in return for an explicit promise or undertaking remains; receipt in return for that undertaking completes the offense without actual performance of the promised act. The official need not deceive the payer by falsely claiming that law authorizes the payment as a fee. Interstate-commerce proof and the beyond-a-reasonable-doubt burden remain unchanged.

**Authority by component:**
- No independent demand and no completed-performance element: [Evans v. United States, “1. No independent demand or initiation element” and “2. The charge requires an exchange; promised performance need not be completed”](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Evans_v_United_States_merits_1992-05-26.md), May 26, 1992; Stevens Parts I–II, joined by White, Blackmun, O'Connor and Souter (five).
- No indispensable false-lawful-entitlement claim: Evans, “3. No false claim of lawful entitlement is indispensable,” May 26, 1992; Stevens Part III, joined by White, Blackmun, Kennedy and Souter (five).

**Present operation effective:** Evans v. United States, May 26, 1992.

**Open question:** Mere gratuities, cases lacking the required undertaking and distinct defenses remain outside the decision. Kennedy's implied-communication account and Stone-Zsela's separate interpretive qualifications do not add Court elements.

**Holdings navigation:** [Evans v. United States, 504 U.S. 255 (1992) — Official-right extortion](C:/Users/alexl/Projects/scotus-career/terms/OT1992/close/HOLDINGS.candidate.md#evans-v-united-states-504-us-255-1992).

### Automatic-death jurors and requested capital voir dire

**Governing question:** Inquiry and qualification of a jury provided by the State to determine a capital sentence.

**Current rule:** A juror who would automatically impose death upon conviction regardless of the facts and required mitigation is subject to challenge for cause. If such a juror serves and death is imposed, that sentence cannot be executed. On the capital defendant's request, voir dire must adequately inquire into that disqualifying view; general assurances of fairness or willingness to obey instructions do not suffice when they leave automatic-death bias unexamined. The inquiry protects impartial consideration and does not require jurors favorable to either side, prescribed mitigation weight or any particular questionnaire.

**Authority by component:**
- Disqualification: [Morgan v. Illinois, “An automatic-death juror is disqualified from capital sentencing”](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Morgan_v_Illinois_merits_1992-06-15.md), June 15, 1992; White, joined by Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy and Souter (six).
- Requested inquiry: Morgan, “Requested inquiry must expose the disqualifying view,” June 15, 1992; same six Justices.

**Present operation effective:** Morgan v. Illinois, June 15, 1992.

**Exceptions and limits:** No constitutional entitlement to jury capital sentencing, rule for unrelated voir dire omissions, new proof quantum, collateral-review rule or retroactivity holding is adopted. Stone's condition requiring jury-only renewed sentencing does not control.

**Holdings navigation:** [Morgan v. Illinois, 504 U.S. 719 (1992) — Capital sentencing](C:/Users/alexl/Projects/scotus-career/terms/OT1992/close/HOLDINGS.candidate.md#morgan-v-illinois-504-us-719-1992).

### Purposeful racial peremptory strikes by the criminal defense

**Governing question:** State objection to a criminal defendant's purposeful exclusion of prospective jurors by race through peremptory challenges.

**Current rule:** The defense's invocation of state peremptory authority to secure judicial exclusion from a governmental jury is state action for that particular act; purposeful racial exclusion violates the juror's equal-protection right. The State may assert that right in its own criminal trial because its tribunal suffers an integrity injury, it has a direct relationship with summoned jurors and jurors face substantial obstacles to separate litigation. When the State establishes a prima facie inference of purposeful discrimination, the defense must give race-neutral explanations and the judge must decide whether the State has proved purposeful discrimination. The ultimate persuasion burden remains on the State. Effective counsel and impartiality confer no right to racial exclusion; genuine race-neutral challenges, actual-bias inquiry and appropriate protection of concrete confidential matters remain available.

**Authority by component:**
- Attribution and prohibition: [Georgia v. McCollum, “1. Purposeful racial defense peremptories are attributable to the State”](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Georgia_v_McCollum_merits_1992-06-18.md), June 18, 1992.
- State's assertion of juror rights: McCollum, “2. The State may assert the excluded jurors' rights,” June 18, 1992.
- Ordinary sequence and defenses: McCollum, “3. Batson's ordinary burdens govern, with defense protections preserved,” June 18, 1992.
- Each component: Blackmun, joined by White, Stevens, Kennedy and Souter (five); Batson's purpose, production and persuasion requirements remain unchanged within the newly covered defense selection act.

**Present operation effective:** Georgia v. McCollum, June 18, 1992.

**Exceptions and limits:** Ordinary defense advice, representation and investigation do not become state action. No explanation is automatically required for every minority-juror strike. Nonracial classifications, unconscious-bias safeguards and particular privilege disputes remain undecided; no general confidential-review procedure is imposed.

**Holdings navigation:** [Georgia v. McCollum, 505 U.S. 42 (1992) — Standing to assert third-party rights](C:/Users/alexl/Projects/scotus-career/terms/OT1992/close/HOLDINGS.candidate.md#georgia-v-mccollum-505-us-42-1992); [Georgia v. McCollum, 505 U.S. 42 (1992) — Racial peremptory challenges](C:/Users/alexl/Projects/scotus-career/terms/OT1992/close/HOLDINGS.candidate.md#georgia-v-mccollum-505-us-42-1992-1).

### Former testimony, similar motive and the Government's evidentiary objection

**Governing question:** Admission of an unavailable witness's prior testimony under Federal Rule of Evidence 804(b)(1), including grand-jury testimony offered against the Government.

**Current rule:** The proponent must establish both an opportunity and a similar motive for the opposing party to develop the prior testimony. The Government's power to immunize a witness at trial and general adversarial fairness do not excuse either requirement; actual proceedings and arguments govern similar motive. Offering independent evidence contradicting the witness's grand-jury account, without presenting any version of that account to the trial jury, does not by itself forfeit the Rule 804(b)(1) objection. Even assuming a privilege-forfeiture analogy, contradiction is not disclosure of the protected communication.

**Authority by component:**
- Complete exception conditions: [United States v. Salerno, “Every condition of the former-testimony exception remains necessary”](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/United_States_v_Salerno_merits_1992-06-19.md), June 19, 1992; Thomas, joined by White, Blackmun, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy and Souter (seven).
- Limited rejection of forfeiture: Salerno, “Contradictory independent evidence does not itself forfeit the objection,” June 19, 1992; same seven Justices.

**Present operation effective:** United States v. Salerno, June 19, 1992.

**Exceptions and limits:** No categorical exclusion of grand-jury testimony, finding of similar motive in the source case, or general classification of hearsay objections as testimonial privileges is established. Other preserved evidentiary and constitutional grounds retain their own requirements.

**Holdings navigation:** [United States v. Salerno, 505 U.S. 317 (1992) — Former testimony](C:/Users/alexl/Projects/scotus-career/terms/OT1992/close/HOLDINGS.candidate.md#united-states-v-salerno-505-us-317-1992).

### Making a firearm before final assembly

**Governing question:** Whether the National Firearms Act's definition of making invariably requires final mechanical assembly.

**Current rule:** The references to manufacturing and otherwise producing in §5845(i) are not confined to the final mechanical act of assembling parts. A universal actual-assembly prerequisite is therefore impermissible. This negative construction supplies no general liability rule for every pure-parts kit, incomplete collection, sham arrangement or genuine lawful alternative configuration.

**Authority by component:**
- Rejection of universal final assembly: [United States v. Thompson/Center Arms Co., “Making is not invariably postponed until final assembly”](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/United_States_v_Thompson_Center_Arms_Co_merits_1992-06-08.md), June 8, 1992; expressly shared proposition of Souter, O'Connor, White, Blackmun, Stevens, Kennedy and Stone-Zsela (seven), independent of their disagreement about the actual package. No complete opinion commands five joins.

**Present operation effective:** United States v. Thompson/Center Arms Co., June 8, 1992.

**Open question:** The broader boundary among parts combinations, intent to assemble and separate criminal offenses remains unresolved. The case-specific dual-use package refund and its application of residual lenity supply no general kit exemption or new lenity method.

**Holdings navigation:** [United States v. Thompson/Center Arms Co., 504 U.S. 505 (1992) — National Firearms Act making tax](C:/Users/alexl/Projects/scotus-career/terms/OT1992/close/HOLDINGS.candidate.md#united-states-v-thompsoncenter-arms-co-504-us-505-1992).

### Abduction and trial under the United States–Mexico Extradition Treaty

**Governing question:** Whether the United States–Mexico Extradition Treaty bars prosecution following a DEA-sponsored forcible transfer protested by Mexico.

**Current rule:** The Treaty does not prohibit that abduction or make extradition the exclusive means of obtaining a defendant's presence. Absent a treaty restriction barring prosecution, Ker–Frisbie permits trial notwithstanding the forcible transfer and protest. Rauscher and Cook remain enforceable when a treaty does supply a relevant restriction.

**Authority by component:**
- Treaty construction and trial consequence: [United States v. Alvarez-Machain, “This Treaty does not bar prosecution after the abduction”](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/United_States_v_Alvarez_Machain_merits_1992-06-15.md), June 15, 1992; White, joined by Scalia, Kennedy, Souter and Thomas (five).

**Present operation effective:** United States v. Alvarez-Machain, June 15, 1992.

**Exceptions and limits:** The holding does not authorize abduction, decide its general international legality, foreclose all other remedies or establish a universal bar to individual treaty enforcement.

**Holdings navigation:** [United States v. Alvarez-Machain, 504 U.S. 655 (1992) — International abduction and treaty limits on prosecution](C:/Users/alexl/Projects/scotus-career/terms/OT1992/close/HOLDINGS.candidate.md#united-states-v-alvarez-machain-504-us-655-1992).

### Prolonged negligent post-accusation delay and presumed defense impairment

**Governing question:** Whether extraordinary governmental negligence after indictment can violate the speedy-trial right without identified trial prejudice, including when the accused was free and unaware of the charge.

**Current rule:** After official accusation, defense impairment remains part of Barker's balance even without incarceration or anxiety. First determine whether delay warrants inquiry; then weigh its duration, reasons, assertion of the right and prejudice together. Sustained governmental negligence weighs less than deliberate obstruction but increasingly against the Government as unjustified delay persists. Extraordinary negligent delay can establish a violation without particularized trial prejudice when the accused did not knowingly acquiesce and the resulting inference of defense impairment is not persuasively rebutted. Possible prejudice alone is insufficient. Once a violation is established, dismissal of the indictment and vacatur of the resulting conviction, rather than sentence adjustment, implement it.

**Authority by component:**

- Scope and the specified negligence/presumed-impairment operation: [Doggett v. United States, “An outstanding indictment protects fair adjudication before arrest” and “Extraordinary negligent delay and unrebutted impairment require dismissal here”](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Doggett_v_United_States_merits_1992-06-24.md), June 24, 1992; Souter's opinion joined by Stone, White, Blackmun, Stevens and Kennedy.
- Dismissal consequence: the second holding applies Strunk without changing that remedy.

**Present operation effective:** *Doggett*, June 24, 1992.

**Exceptions and limits:** Diligence, justified obstacles, evasion, knowing acquiescence or materially shorter neglect may yield a different balance. No fixed deadline, automatic constitutional-violation presumption, general transfer of the ultimate constitutional burden, preaccusation rule or independent right of repose is adopted.

**Holdings navigation:** [Doggett v. United States, 505 U.S. 647 (1992) — Speedy trial](C:/Users/alexl/Projects/scotus-career/terms/OT1992/close/HOLDINGS.candidate.md#doggett-v-united-states-505-us-647-1992-1).

### Defendant's preponderance burden and equivalent competence presumption

**Governing question:** Whether California may allocate persuasion concerning present trial incompetence to the defendant.

**Current rule:** At an adequate competency hearing with counsel and access to psychiatric evidence, due process permits requiring the defendant asserting incompetence to prove it by a preponderance. California's presumption of competence is permissible only insofar as it restates that allocation, deciding evenly balanced evidence rather than supplying independent psychiatric proof. Sufficient doubt still requires inquiry; it does not itself establish incompetence. Neither rule permits trial of a defendant established to be incompetent or changes the functional competence standard.

**Authority by component:**

- Burden and presumption: [Medina v. California, “Defendant's preponderance burden at a competency hearing” and “The associated presumption states the same permitted burden”](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Medina_v_California_merits_1992-06-22.md), June 22, 1992. Kennedy, White, Scalia and Thomas in the principal opinion and O'Connor and Souter in the judgment concurrence expressly support both narrow propositions.

**Present operation effective:** *Medina*, June 22, 1992.

**Exceptions and limits:** No whole opinion commands a majority. No categorical exclusion of Mathews from criminal procedure, higher proof quantum, irrebuttable presumption or additional evidentiary weight follows. Insanity, offense-element proof and the feasibility of retrospective competence determinations remain distinct.

**Holdings navigation:** [Medina v. California, 505 U.S. 437 (1992) — Competency hearings](C:/Users/alexl/Projects/scotus-career/terms/OT1992/close/HOLDINGS.candidate.md#medina-v-california-505-us-437-1992).

### Capital eligibility innocence as a gateway to barred habeas claims

**Governing question:** Review of successive, abusive or procedurally defaulted capital habeas claims without otherwise required cause and prejudice.

**Current rule:** The prisoner must show by clear and convincing evidence that, absent constitutional error, no reasonable juror would find the prisoner eligible for death under applicable state law. Examine the offense, required aggravating facts and every other necessary eligibility condition. Additional mitigation affecting only the discretionary selection of death does not satisfy this gateway. Opening the gateway permits consideration of an otherwise barred claim; it does not itself establish the claim or grant relief.

**Authority by component:**

- Eligibility-only scope, no-reasonable-juror inquiry and clear-and-convincing burden: [Sawyer v. Whitley, “Capital innocence as a gateway to otherwise barred claims”](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Sawyer_v_Whitley_merits_1992-06-22.md), June 22, 1992; Kennedy's opinion joined by White, Scalia, Souter and Thomas. Broader judgment concurrences supply no component.

**Present operation effective:** *Sawyer*, June 22, 1992.

**Exceptions and limits:** Ordinary cause and prejudice, exhaustion, retroactivity, the merits standards and proper factual-development rules remain distinct. Neither ordinary sentencing prejudice nor discretionary mitigation replaces this gateway. The crime-innocence standard and freestanding innocence claims are not resolved here. No categorical exclusion of impeachment evidence follows from its insufficiency on Sawyer's record.

**Holdings navigation:** [Sawyer v. Whitley, 505 U.S. 333 (1992) — Habeas procedural gateways](C:/Users/alexl/Projects/scotus-career/terms/OT1992/close/HOLDINGS.candidate.md#sawyer-v-whitley-505-us-333-1992).

### Factually unsupported lawful grounds in general verdicts and capital recommendations

**Governing question:** The effect of an evidentially unsupported but legally available ground when a general decision does not identify which ground was used.

**Current rule:** A general multiple-object federal conspiracy verdict need not be set aside merely because one lawful object lacks sufficient evidence if another is sufficiently proved under the unchanged beyond-a-reasonable-doubt standard. Factual insufficiency is distinct from legal invalidity; this rule does not preserve a verdict resting on a legally forbidden theory. In the separate Florida advisory-capital-jury setting, submission of a legally available but evidentially unsupported aggravator does not alone establish reliance when the general recommendation identifies no aggravator findings. That inference boundary neither finds actual rejection of the factor nor resolves a legally invalid instruction. Express judicial reliance remains distinct and subject to capital-weighing correction.

**Authority by component:**

- General multiple-object conspiracy verdict and factual/legal distinction: [Griffin v. United States, “General verdict containing a factually unsupported lawful object”](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Griffin_v_United_States_merits_1991-12-03.md), December 3, 1991; Scalia, Stone, White, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy and Souter.
- Limited extension to an unparticularized advisory capital recommendation: [Sochor v. Florida, “The general jury recommendation does not establish coldness reliance”](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Sochor_v_Florida_merits_1992-06-08.md), June 8, 1992; Souter's Part III-A joined by White, O'Connor, Kennedy and Thomas.

**Present operation effective:** *Sochor*, June 8, 1992, for the advisory-recommendation component; *Griffin*, December 3, 1991, remains the operative authority for the conspiracy-verdict component.

**Exceptions and limits:** Failure of proof on every route, an element-diluting instruction, an independently presented unanimity defect and broader capital-jury error rules are not resolved. No fact-specific jury finding may be supplied from a general recommendation.

**Related current rules:** “Invalid aggravators in capital weighing and correction of sentencing error” governs constitutionally defective weighing and the influence of an inadequately narrowed jury instruction; the factual-insufficiency rule does not displace it.

**Holdings navigation:** [Griffin v. United States, 502 U.S. 46 (1991) — General verdicts](C:/Users/alexl/Projects/scotus-career/terms/OT1992/close/HOLDINGS.candidate.md#griffin-v-united-states-502-us-46-1991); [Sochor v. Florida, 504 U.S. 527 (1992) — Capital sentencing](C:/Users/alexl/Projects/scotus-career/terms/OT1992/close/HOLDINGS.candidate.md#sochor-v-florida-504-us-527-1992-1).

### Invalid aggravators in capital weighing and correction of sentencing error

**Governing question:** Constitutionally invalid aggravation in a weighing State, whether used directly by the judge or transmitted through a jury recommendation carrying legally required great weight.

**Current rule:** Remaining valid aggravators do not alone cure a constitutional weighing error, although an invalid weighing factor does not automatically negate death eligibility if valid eligibility findings remain. The state judicial system must provide genuine reweighing without the invalid factor where state law authorizes it, or constitutional harmless-error review proving beyond a reasonable doubt that the error did not affect the sentence. Bare factor counting, proportionality review or a harmless-error label supplies neither cure; no particular verbal formula is indispensable if the substantive finding is actually made. A jury's inadequately narrowed aggravating instruction can infect the final death sentence when the judge must give the resulting recommendation great weight, even if the judge directly finds only valid aggravators. Correction must address that transmitted influence. If no lawful cure is available, a new sentencing proceeding is required. Neither automatic life imprisonment nor disturbance of guilt follows.

**Authority by component:**

- Distinct weighing error and eligibility, with lawful correction routes: [Stringer v. Black, “A weighing error needs a constitutionally adequate cure”](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Stringer_v_Black_merits_1992-03-09.md), March 9, 1992; Kennedy for Stone, White, Blackmun, Stevens and O'Connor.
- Actual judicial reliance, substantive cure and no magic-words requirement: [Sochor v. Florida, “The judge's documented coldness reliance requires correction” and “The existing appellate explanation does not cure the error”](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Sochor_v_Florida_merits_1992-06-08.md), June 8, 1992; respectively the eight-Justice Part III-B-1 and six-Justice Parts III-B-2/IV, applying Stringer, Clemons, Parker and Chapman without changing their correction standard.
- A legally influential recommendation and correction of its transmitted effect: [Espinosa v. Florida, “A legally influential recommendation cannot rest on an inadequately narrowed aggravator” and “Correction must address the recommendation's influence on the death sentence”](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Espinosa_v_Florida_summary_merits_1992-06-29.md), June 29, 1992; per curiam joined by Stone, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter and Thomas.

**Present operation effective:** *Espinosa*, June 29, 1992, extending the rule to the recommendation's legally required influence. The earlier direct-weighing rule remains supplied by *Stringer*, March 9, 1992.

**Burdens and proof:** On the harmless-error route the State bears Chapman's beyond-a-reasonable-doubt burden. That requirement is distinct from authority to perform a genuine reweighing and from collateral-review gateways.

**Exceptions and limits:** States retain latitude in assigning sentencing roles, but must give constitutional guidance to an influential recommendation. No factor-specific juror finding is presumed, and constitutional permission for a kind of appellate cure does not establish that state law authorizes it. Independent preservation, default and habeas barriers remain intact.

**Related current rules:** “Factually unsupported lawful grounds in general verdicts and capital recommendations” preserves the distinct inference boundary for a legally available but evidentially unsupported jury factor. “Capital eligibility innocence as a gateway to barred habeas claims” governs access to review of otherwise barred claims, not correction of an available claim's weighing error.

**Holdings navigation:** [Stringer v. Black, 503 U.S. 222 (1992) — Capital sentencing and habeas retroactivity](C:/Users/alexl/Projects/scotus-career/terms/OT1992/close/HOLDINGS.candidate.md#stringer-v-black-503-us-222-1992); [Sochor v. Florida, 504 U.S. 527 (1992) — Capital sentencing](C:/Users/alexl/Projects/scotus-career/terms/OT1992/close/HOLDINGS.candidate.md#sochor-v-florida-504-us-527-1992-1); [Espinosa v. Florida, 505 U.S. 1079 (1992) — Capital sentencing](C:/Users/alexl/Projects/scotus-career/terms/OT1992/close/HOLDINGS.candidate.md#espinosa-v-florida-505-us-1079-1992).

### Collateral regularity of prior counseled pleas used for enhancement

**Governing question:** What instructions govern collateral regularity of prior counseled pleas used for enhancement?

**Current rule:** For collateral challenges to final counseled convictions used for sentence enhancement, due process permits Kentucky's procedure requiring the Commonwealth to prove the judgment and the defendant to produce evidence of infringement or procedural irregularity. After rebuttal, Kentucky requires the Commonwealth affirmatively to establish constitutional validity. Missing old transcripts alone, without alleged official misconduct, do not rebut regularity. Due process does not require clear-and-convincing extrarecord proof of plea validity in that setting. This is permission for that allocation, not a universal collateral proof quantum; direct-review waiver requirements and the exclusion of invalid uncounseled convictions survive.

**Authority by component:**

- Holding 1 — initial production burden.: [Parke v. Raley — Holding 1 — initial production burden.](C:/Users/alexl/Projects/scotus-career/terms/OT1992/records/parke-v-raley-merits-1992-12-01.md), 1992-12-01. O’Connor’s opinion for the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, White, Stevens, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter and Thomas; eight votes at this level. Blackmun agrees with the result in a separate writing limited to sentencing.
- Holding 2 — measure of proof.: [Parke v. Raley — Holding 2 — measure of proof.](C:/Users/alexl/Projects/scotus-career/terms/OT1992/records/parke-v-raley-merits-1992-12-01.md), 1992-12-01. The same eight-Justice Court opinion; Blackmun independently agrees on his sentencing ground.

**Present operation effective:** 1992-12-01; Parke v. Raley, for the components identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** [Parke v. Raley, 506 U.S. 20 (1992)](C:/Users/alexl/Projects/scotus-career/terms/OT1992/close/HOLDINGS.candidate.md#parke-v-raley-506-us-20-1992).

### Possessory seizures and the claimant's own Fourth Amendment interest

**Governing question:** What instructions govern possessory seizures and the claimant's own Fourth Amendment interest?

**Current rule:** Meaningful government-attributable interference with possession of a home is a Fourth Amendment seizure without a search, privacy or liberty invasion, or criminal investigative purpose. A competing due-process characterization does not displace the specific seizure claim; coverage does not establish unreasonableness or §1983 liability. A suppression claimant must show interference with his or her own protected property interest in a seizure or reasonable expectation of privacy in a search. Conspiracy membership neither creates an exception to that personal-interest requirement nor destroys an actual protected interest. Attribution, immunity and the remaining merits must be separately established.

**Authority by component:**

- A seizure of a home protects possession independently of privacy: [Soldal v. Cook County — A seizure of a home protects possession independently of privacy](C:/Users/alexl/Projects/scotus-career/terms/OT1992/records/Soldal_v_Cook_County_merits_1992-12-08.md), 1992-12-08. White’s opinion, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas; nine Justices adopt the same rule.
- A due-process characterization does not displace the specific seizure claim: [Soldal v. Cook County — A due-process characterization does not displace the specific seizure claim](C:/Users/alexl/Projects/scotus-career/terms/OT1992/records/Soldal_v_Cook_County_merits_1992-12-08.md), 1992-12-08. White’s opinion, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas; unanimous holding.
- Suppression depends on the defendant’s own protected interest: [United States v. Padilla — Suppression depends on the defendant’s own protected interest](C:/Users/alexl/Projects/scotus-career/terms/OT1992/records/padilla_merits_1993-05-03.md), 1993-05-03. Per curiam Opinion of the Court; Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (9 Justices). Direct majority agreement at this level of generality; no Marks inference.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-05-03; Soldal v. Cook County; United States v. Padilla, for the components identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** [Soldal v. Cook County, 506 U.S. 56 (1992)](C:/Users/alexl/Projects/scotus-career/terms/OT1992/close/HOLDINGS.candidate.md#soldal-v-cook-county-506-us-56-1992); [United States v. Padilla, 508 U.S. 77 (1993)](C:/Users/alexl/Projects/scotus-career/terms/OT1992/close/HOLDINGS.candidate.md#united-states-v-padilla-508-us-77-1993).

### Initial presence before voluntary-absence trial continuation

**Governing question:** What instructions govern initial presence before voluntary-absence trial continuation?

**Current rule:** Rule 43's voluntary-absence continuation exception requires initial presence when trial begins; knowing voluntary pretrial absence does not authorize beginning trial without the defendant under that exception. Other enumerated exceptions and independent lawful flight sanctions retain their terms. The rule does not decide the constitutional minimum or establish a general harmless-error doctrine.

**Authority by component:**

- Trial cannot begin under the voluntary-absence exception without initial presence: [Crosby v. United States — Trial cannot begin under the voluntary-absence exception without initial presence](C:/Users/alexl/Projects/scotus-career/terms/OT1992/records/Crosby_v_United_States_merits_1993-01-13.md), 1993-01-13. Blackmun’s opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, White, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas; nine participating Justices adopt the rule and explanation.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-01-13; Crosby v. United States, for the components identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** [Crosby v. United States, 506 U.S. 255 (1993)](C:/Users/alexl/Projects/scotus-career/terms/OT1992/close/HOLDINGS.candidate.md#crosby-v-united-states-506-us-255-1993).

### Recovered capital transcripts and law-of-the-case review

**Governing question:** What instructions govern recovered capital transcripts and law-of-the-case review?

**Current rule:** An appellate habeas court may not unjustifiably exclude a recovered capital-sentencing transcript materially contradicting the factual account underlying its prior decision and then use that exclusion to defeat its own manifest-injustice exception to law of the case. Delay substantially caused by erroneous State representations that no transcript existed does not justify that exclusion. Proper further consideration remains subject to independent claim bars and the ordinary ineffective-assistance merits; contradiction does not itself establish prejudice or a general capital exemption from procedural rules.

**Authority by component:**

- Accurate transcript before law-of-the-case review: [Dobbs v. Zant — Accurate transcript before law-of-the-case review](C:/Users/alexl/Projects/scotus-career/terms/OT1992/records/Dobbs_v_Zant_summary_merits_1993-01-19.md), 1993-01-19. The per curiam opinion, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy and Souter; six of nine participating Justices adopt this rule and the remand. Scalia and Thomas concur only in the judgment; White would use plenary review.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-01-19; Dobbs v. Zant, for the components identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** [Dobbs v. Zant, 506 U.S. 357 (1993)](C:/Users/alexl/Projects/scotus-career/terms/OT1992/close/HOLDINGS.candidate.md#dobbs-v-zant-506-us-357-1993).

### Effective capital consideration of independent culpability mitigation

**Governing question:** What instructions govern effective capital consideration of independent culpability mitigation?

**Current rule:** When record-supported mitigation has material culpability significance that the authorized sentencing answers cannot express, a reasonable likelihood that the charge, evidence and trial as a whole prevent that use requires an effective corrective instruction or procedure. Admission and partial relevance to future danger are insufficient if a truthful finding of deliberateness and danger prevents giving effect to youth's independent mitigating force. Disturbed upbringing need not involve a uniquely severe permanent handicap or lack any relation to future danger. The sentencer need not give favorable weight, and mere possibility of a restrictive reading is insufficient. Jurek's conditional approval, Lockett and Eddings dictated this functional requirement before Graham's September 10, 1984 finality; no new Teague exception follows. Adequate capital resentencing or a lawful noncapital sentence can provide correction without disturbing guilt. No general positive-character rule follows from Graham's separate fractured component.

Parts I–III and V of Souter's opinion, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens and O'Connor; five Justices adopt this prefinality-law holding, its application to youth and background, and the conditional sentencing remedy.
- Youth requires an effective route for its independent mitigating force: [Graham v. Collins — Youth requires an effective route for its independent mitigating force](C:/Users/alexl/Projects/scotus-career/terms/OT1992/records/Graham_v_Collins_merits_1993-01-25.md), 1993-01-25. Parts II and V of Souter's opinion, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens and O'Connor; five Justices adopt this independently sufficient ground for sentencing relief and its conditional remedy.
- The submitted background evidence also requires effective consideration: [Graham v. Parts III and V of Souter's opinion, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens and O'Connor; five Justices adopt this independent background-evidence ground and the conditional sentencing remedy.

### Loss of a displaced substantive rule and counsel prejudice

**Governing question:** What instructions govern loss of a displaced substantive rule and counsel prejudice?

**Current rule:** An omitted objection does not establish Strickland prejudice when its lost benefit is a substantive rule subsequently rejected, governing law recognizes no entitlement to that benefit, and the omission otherwise deprived the defendant of no fair or reliable sentencing protection. Ordinary reasonable-probability analysis remains; the usual claim acquires no additional unfairness requirement. Performance is assessed from counsel's perspective at the time. Teague does not require disregarding a subsequent legal correction favorable to the State for this limited prejudice inquiry. No actual-innocence prerequisite or general harmless-error rule follows.

Loss of a displaced substantive rule is not cognizable prejudice here](C:/Users/alexl/Projects/scotus-career/terms/OT1992/records/Lockhart_v_Fretwell_merits_1993-01-25.md), 1993-01-25. O'Connor's opinion of the Court, joined by White, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter and Thomas; six Justices adopt the rule and its application to the Collins objection.
- 2. Teague does not preserve the displaced rule for this prejudice inquiry: [Lockhart v. Fretwell — 2. Teague does not preserve the displaced rule for this prejudice inquiry](C:/Users/alexl/Projects/scotus-career/terms/OT1992/records/Lockhart_v_Fretwell_merits_1993-01-25.md), 1993-01-25. O'Connor's opinion of the Court, joined by White, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter and Thomas; six Justices adopt this answer to the temporal objection.

### Tailored relief for prejudice in properly joined trials

**Governing question:** What instructions govern tailored relief for prejudice in properly joined trials?

**Current rule:** Mutually antagonistic defenses do not alone require severance under Rule 14. For properly joined defendants, the district court considers serious risk to a specific trial right or a reliable individualized verdict and selects effective relief in its sound discretion, reviewed for abuse of discretion. Instructions or safeguards may cure prejudice without severance, but are not presumed to cure every risk. Joinder, confrontation and proof beyond reasonable doubt retain their independent requirements.
